The steps of the migration in the order they can be taken: the execution plan (execution-plan.json) laid out in ten waves. A step depends on the steps in its row and on no other; a wave is a starting point for the schedule, not a schedule.

The plan holds 339 steps: 49 automated (31 free of decisions and manual prerequisites, 1 of them can run as written) and 288 manual (a person can start 120 now); of all the steps, 2 after a manual prerequisite (1 of them manual), 5 after an automated step, 96 waiting for a decision, 83 not executable whatever is decided and 2 refused.

Steps: partially executable. Execution completeness: COMPLETE (each of the 288 manual steps states what it is, why, who carries it out and when it is done, and the input it needs where it depends on one). 31 of the 49 automated steps (OpenTofu or a script) are free of decisions and manual prerequisites (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 17 require a design decision) · 1 can run as written · 30 of those 31 wait for a value (project_id, carts_service_task_project_roles, catalog_service_task_project_roles, checkout_service_task_project_roles and 21 more); a person can start 120 of the 288 manual steps now (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 5 wait for an automated step, 79 require a design decision, 83 are not executable as the plan stands); 2 steps of the plan are permanently unsupported (refused); 1 step of the plan has its verification incomplete: it carries critical settings of the source that no check validates, which a person confirms in the draft. Migration-ready: NO — 309 critical settings of 50 source resources have manual or refused steps open (307 manual, 2 refused); 181 steps of the plan cannot be carried out now (96 wait only for a decision, 83 are not executable whatever is decided, 2 are refused).

This roadmap holds the steps of the execution plan only. A block of the Terraform draft that no plan lists is not a step here; the draft in terraform/ is the whole of what is written, and technical-sequencing.md orders every source unit by its dependencies.

answer with one of its options: "Place the cluster and the servers in one STACKIT network, and admit the clients of the managed services by access lists naming its range" or "Keep the drafted networks and place each consumer in the network of its tier" or "Redesign the network placement of the workloads" or "Accept that the drafted network holds none of the source's consumers of its subnet" | 3 steps of the plan |
| `DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST` | Decide the egress address of module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private: keep the changing address of the STACKIT network, or give each server that an allow-list names a STACKIT public IP | a NAT gateway's fixed egress address is lost on STACKIT: the address a routed network leaves by is the network's own, shared by every routed network of the project, not fixed and not settable | module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private | before the pilot; answer with one of its options: "Accept the address STACKIT assigns to the network for outbound traffic" or "Give each server that an allow-list names a STACKIT public IP" | 8 steps of the plan |
| `DEC-OPS-STACKITLOGS-RETENTION-OVER-180-DAYS` | Decide whether the logs of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks older than 180 days are kept in an archive outside the Logs instance | the source keeps its logs for ever and a STACKIT Logs instance keeps at most 180 days, so the instance drafted for it (stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d) holds only the last 180 days; logs older than that exist only if a person configures an archive beside it. Choose the archive if the workload needs them; reject it if the requirement is that more than 180 days stay inside STACKIT Logs with no external archive, which STACKIT Logs alone cannot meet | module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks | before the cutover; answer with one of its options: "Keep the logs for the 180 days of the Logs instance only" or "For the period beyond 180 days, also stream the logs to long-term storage" | 2 steps of the plan |
| not in the decision register | Decide which solution carries module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.ecs_events (Event-pattern rule: routes the events that match a pattern to its targets): choose one of the candidate solutions the plan lists for it, or design it another way and leave it to a person | the target has no equal of this Event-pattern rule: routes the events that match a pattern to its targets: The events a source service emits about itself do not exist on STACKIT. The producers publish their own events (for example to RabbitMQ), and a consumer on a Kubernetes cluster applies the selection and calls the target. To decide: Which events still matter and who publishes them, the topic or exchange, where the consumer runs and how events are selected. | module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.ecs_events | before the pilot | 2 steps of the plan |

## Texts the steps share

A text that many steps share word for word is written once here, with what is the step's own in braces (`{name}` for the name of a resource, `{address}` for an IP address or network). The What and Can start cells of a step name the wording of what it does (W) and the state it is in (S), and the first of them gives, after the number, what stands for the braces.

- **S1** (39 steps of the plan): Not ready: StackToIT performs none of this: it deletes nothing in any cloud, and no step of this plan automates the retirement; it also waits for 18 decisions: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN` and 17 more (decision group `BS-eb9126787384` of execution-plan.json)

## Wave 4 — Platform

Kubernetes, load balancers, logging and the other shared services.

The manual steps of this wave in 11 work packages (`execution/manual/actions.md` lists every package with its steps):

| Package | What | Owner | Steps | Affected resources | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WP-20` | Set up the log delivery and its labels (platform services) | Platform Architect | 3 | 1 | waits for decisions (1 of 3 manual steps) |
| `WP-21` | Decide and set the critical settings (platform services) | Identity Architect | 7 | 7 | a person can start it now |
| `WP-22` | Decide and set the critical settings (platform services) | Network Architect | 7 | 7 | a person can start it now |
| `WP-23` | Decide and set the critical settings (platform services) | Platform Architect | 7 | 7 | a person can start it now |
| `WP-24` | Decide and set the critical settings (platform services) | Security Architect | 6 | 6 | a person can start it now |
| `WP-25` | Set up by hand the resources the draft writes nothing for (platform services) | Platform Architect | 1 | 1 | a person can start it now |
| `WP-26` | Decide on the switched-off modules that hold a STACKIT alternative of what has no equal (platform services) | Platform Architect | 1 | 1 | waits for decisions |
| `WP-27` | Cluster access (platform services) | Platform Architect | 1 | 6 | a person can start it after the automated step it comes after has run |
| `WP-28` | Platform credential (platform services) | Identity Architect | 15 | 5 | a person can start it now |
| `WP-29` | Secrets migration user retire (platform services) | Security Architect | 1 | 4 | waits for decisions |
| `WP-30` | Secrets read back (platform services) | Security Architect | 1 | 4 | waits for decisions |

| Package | What | Owner | Steps | Affected resources | State |

| `WP-19` | Move the data and the objects to the target | Data Architect | 2 | 1 | waits for decisions |
| `WP-31` | Move the data and the objects to the target | Data Architect | 10 | 3 | waits for decisions |
| `WP-34` | Move the data and the objects to the target | Data Architect | 22 | 5 | waits for decisions |
| `WP-35` | Move the data and the objects to the target | Migration Owner | 10 | 5 | waits for decisions |

The steps of the wave, the manual ones among them:

| Step | What | AWS resource | Mode | Depends on | Can start |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MAN/data/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default/preflight` | Close what the plan cannot decide yet, from the move host, with read-only commands; no password goes on a command line or into the history of the shell. Source (`valkey-cli -h <source-host> -p <source-port>`): `INFO server` (the engine and its version), `INFO keyspace` and `DBSIZE` (the keys and the keys with an expiry; on every primary when cluster mode is on, listed with `CLUSTER NODES`), `INFO memory` (used_memory and maxmemory), `SCAN 0 COUNT 100` with `TYPE`, `DUMP` and `PTTL` of a few keys of each family (the commands of the copy; the types the copy has no branch for show here), and the credential the copy signs in with. Target (after the draft is applied and the instance and a service key exist; `valkey-cli -h <target-host> -p <target-port> --tls --sni <target-host> --user <target-user> --askpass`, where `--askpass` makes the client ask for the password of the service key at its prompt): `INFO server` (Valkey 8), `DBSIZE` (0) and `INFO memory`; do not expect `CONFIG`, `ACL` or `MODULE`, which a probe found denied to the service key. Check that the move host is in `parameters.sgw_acl` of the instance (remove it afterwards), reaches both ends, and that the clients of the cache are listed with the TLS and the credential each uses. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/cache-dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default/data-move.md#preflight`. | module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default | Manual | `TF/stackit_valkey_credential.dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default`, `TF/stackit_valkey_instance.dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default` | Blocked by `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default` |
| `MAN/data/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default/rehearsal` | Rehearse the copy on a sample into a scratch Key Value Store instance of the same plan and version as the draft's instance (a second instance, deleted afterwards: the real instance must stay empty for the final copy): copy the keys of a prefix or a few thousand keys of every family with the client of the copy (`SCAN` with `MATCH`, `DUMP`, `PTTL`, `RESTORE` with the remaining time to live), note the keys a second, the bytes a second and the keys RESTORE refuses, point one copy of the application at the scratch instance with TLS and run its own test suite, and compute the freeze window from the throughput: keys / (keys a second) is the lower bound of the copy. Delete the scratch instance afterwards. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/cache-dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default/data-move.md#rehearsal`. | module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default | Manual | `MAN/data/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default/preflight` | Blocked by `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default` |
| `MAN/data/module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this/preflight` | Close what the plan cannot decide yet, from the move host, with read-only statements; no password goes on a command line (the client prompts for it). Source (`mysql --host=<source-host> --port=<source-port> --user=<source-user> --password`): `SELECT VERSION();`, `SELECT table_schema, table_collation, COUNT(*) FROM information_schema.tables GROUP BY 1, 2;` and the same over `information_schema.columns` for the collations the columns use, `SELECT routine_schema, routine_type, COUNT(*) FROM information_schema.routines GROUP BY 1, 2;` and the triggers, events and views the same way, the DEFINER accounts (`SELECT DISTINCT definer FROM information_schema.routines`, and for the views and events), `SELECT user, host, plugin FROM mysql.user ORDER BY 1, 2;` and `SHOW GRANTS FOR '<user>'@'<host>';` for each (names and plugins, never the authentication string), `SELECT @@GLOBAL.sql_mode, @@GLOBAL.time_zone, @@GLOBAL.gtid_mode;`, `SHOW DATABASES;` and the engines and the tables without a primary key (the queries of the check table-engines-and-keys). Target (after the draft is applied and the instance and its service key exist; `mysql --host=<target-host> --port=<target-port> --user=<target-user> --password --database=<target-database>`): `SELECT VERSION();`, `SELECT @@GLOBAL.sql_mode, @@GLOBAL.time_zone;`, `SHOW VARIABLES LIKE 'character_set%';`, `SHOW VARIABLES LIKE 'collation%';`, `SHOW COLLATION WHERE Collation LIKE 'utf8mb4%0900%';` and `SHOW GRANTS;`. Check that the move host is in `parameters.sgw_acl` of the instance (remove it afterwards), reaches both ends and uses the custom port of the instance's service key. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/database-dependencies_catalog_rds_this/data-move.md#preflight`. | module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this | Manual | `TF/stackit_mariadb_credential.dependencies_catalog_rds_this`, `TF/stackit_mariadb_instance.dependencies_catalog_rds_this` | Blocked by `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this` |

Check that the move host is in `parameters.sgw_acl` of the instance (remove it afterwards), reaches both management APIs, and that the clients of the broker are listed with the protocol, the library and the credentials each uses. ## Wave 7 — Cutover

Moving traffic to the target and the steps that must be taken before production.

The manual steps of this wave in 5 work packages (`execution/manual/actions.md` lists every package with its steps):

| Package | What | Owner | Steps | Affected resources | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WP-36` | Switch the data consumers to the target | Data Architect | 4 | 4 | waits for decisions |
| `WP-37` | Switch the data consumers to the target | Identity Architect | 4 | 4 | waits for decisions |
| `WP-38` | Confirm that the cutover can start and that it is complete | Migration Owner | 2 | 0 | not executable as the plan stands (1 of 2 manual steps) |
| `WP-39` | Switch the clients to the target | Network Architect | 8 | 8 | not executable as the plan stands (7 of 8 manual steps) |
| `WP-40` | Switch the workloads and the message producers and consumers to the target | Platform Architect | 7 | 6 | not executable as the plan stands (5 of 7 manual steps) |

The steps of the wave, the manual ones among them:

| Step | What | AWS resource | Mode | Depends on | Can start |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MAN/cutover/readiness` | Confirm that every workload step and every data move of the plan is done (the steps this one follows) before the first switch of the cutover: the plan does not know which workloads and data stores each entry point serves, so every switch waits for all of them | — | Manual | 23 steps of the plan: `MAN/data/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq/preflight` and 22 more (every predecessor is in execution-plan.json depends_on) | Blocked by 17 decisions: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` and 16 more (decision group `BS-6d37814a1c92` of execution-plan.json) |

| `MAN/cutover/messaging-drain/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` | Drain the queues of the AWS broker module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (aws_mq_broker): its consumers go on reading them, with no producer sending to the broker any more, until every queue holds no message. Read the depth in CloudWatch: Amazon MQ for RabbitMQ publishes MessageCount (the sum of the ready and the unacknowledged messages) for the broker and, on 3.x brokers, for each queue (the dimensions Queue and VirtualHost; on 4.x brokers the figures of a queue come from the Prometheus endpoint), and Amazon MQ for ActiveMQ publishes QueueSize and InFlightCount for each queue and TotalMessageCount for the broker. On a RabbitMQ broker the management API gives the same figure for every queue: `curl -s -u "<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "<source-broker-endpoint>/api/queues" \| jq '[.[] \| (.messages_ready // 0) + (.messages_unacknowledged // 0)] \| add'` is 0 when they are empty. An inactive durable topic subscriber (InactiveDurableTopicSubscribersCount) holds messages that only its own client can read: connect it to the AWS broker until it has read them. The plan does not read the names of the queues of the broker | module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq | Manual | `MAN/cutover/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` | Blocked by 17 decisions: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` and 16 more (decision group `BS-6d37814a1c92` of execution-plan.json) |
